40.             ResourceMap stringResourcesResourceMap = ResourceManager.Current.MainResourceMap.GetSubtree("Resources");

42.         }
43. 
44.         private void LanguageOverrideCombo_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
45.         {
46.             var combo = sender as ComboBox;
47.             var item = combo.SelectedValue as ComboBoxItem;
48.             var languageTag = item.Tag as string;
49. 
50.             // Ignore the divider (tag = "-")
51.             if (languageTag == "-")
52.             {
53.                 combo.SelectedIndex = lastSelectedIndex;
54.             }
55.             else
56.             {
57.                 lastSelectedIndex = combo.SelectedIndex;
58. 
59.                 // Set the persistent language override
60.                 ApplicationLanguages.PrimaryLanguageOverride = languageTag;
61. 
62.                 // update current app languages message
63.                 UpdateCurrentAppLanguageMessage();
64.             }
65.         }
66. 
67.         void AddItemForLanguageTag(string languageTag)
68.         {
69.             var language = new Language(languageTag);
70.             var item = new ComboBoxItem { Content = language.DisplayName, Tag = languageTag };
71.             LanguageOverrideCombo.Items.Add(item);
72. 
73.             // Select this item if it is the primary language override.
74.             if (languageTag == ApplicationLanguages.PrimaryLanguageOverride)
75.             {
76.                 LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectedItem = item;
77.             }
78.         }
79. 
80.         void PopulateComboBox()
81.         {
82.             // First show the default setting
83.             LanguageOverrideCombo.Items.Add(new ComboBoxItem { Content = "Use language preferences (recommended)", Tag = "" });
84.             LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectedIndex = 0;
85. 
86.             // If there are app languages that the user speaks, show them next
87. 
88.             // Note: the first (non-override) language, if set as the primary language override
89.             // would give the same result as not having any primary language override. There's
90.             // still a difference, though: If the user changes their language preferences, the 
91.             // default setting (no override) would mean that the actual primary app language
92.             // could change. But if it's set as an override, then it will remain the primary
93.             // app language after the user changes their language preferences.
94. 
95.             foreach (var languageTag in ApplicationLanguages.Languages)
96.             {
97.                 AddItemForLanguageTag(languageTag);
98.             }
99. 
100.             // Now add a divider followed by all the app manifest languages (in case the user
101.             // wants to pick a language not currently in their profile)
102. 
103.             // NOTE: If an app is deployed using a bundle with resource packages, the following
104.             // addition to the list may not be useful: The set of languages returned by 
105.             // ApplicationLanguages.ManifestLanguages will consist of only those manifest 
106.             // languages in the main app package or in the resource packages that are installed 
107.             // and registered for the current user. Language resource packages get deployed for 
108.             // the user if the language is in the user's profile. Therefore, the only difference 
109.             // from the set returned by ApplicationLanguages.Languages above would depend on 
110.             // which languages are included in the main app package.
111. 
112.             LanguageOverrideCombo.Items.Add(new ComboBoxItem { Content = "——————", Tag = "-" });
113. 
114.             // In a production app, this list should be sorted, but that's beyond the
115.             // focus of this sample.
116.             foreach (var languageTag in ApplicationLanguages.ManifestLanguages)
117.             {
118.                 AddItemForLanguageTag(languageTag);
119.             }
120. 
121.             LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectionChanged += LanguageOverrideCombo_SelectionChanged;
122. 
123.             lastSelectedIndex = LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectedIndex;
124.         }
125.

129.         }

52.             Button b = sender as Button;
53.             if (b != null)
54.             {
55.                 // use a cloned context for this scenario so that qualifier values set
56.                 // in this scenario don't impact behaviour in other scenarios that
57.                 // use a default context for the view (crossover effects between
58.                 // the scenarios will not be expected)
59.                 // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#getforcurrentview
60.                 var context = ResourceContext.GetForCurrentView().Clone();
61. 
62.                 var selectedLanguage = Scenario10ComboBox_Language.SelectedValue;
63.                 var selectedScale = Scenario10ComboBox_Scale.SelectedValue;
64.                 var selectedContrast = Scenario10ComboBox_Contrast.SelectedValue;
65.                 var selectedHomeRegion = Scenario10ComboBox_HomeRegion.SelectedValue;
66.

68.                 {
69.                     context.QualifierValues["language"] = selectedLanguage.ToString();
70.                 }
71.                 if (selectedScale != null)
72.                 {
73.                     context.QualifierValues["scale"] = selectedScale.ToString();
74.                 }
75.                 if (selectedContrast != null)
76.                 {
77.                     context.QualifierValues["contrast"] = selectedContrast.ToString();
78.                 }
79.                 if (selectedHomeRegion != null)
80.                 {
81.                     context.QualifierValues["homeregion"] = selectedHomeRegion.ToString();
82.                 }
83. 
84.                 Scenario10_SearchMultipleResourceIds(context, new string[] { "LanguageOnly", "ScaleOnly", "ContrastOnly", "HomeRegionOnly", "MultiDimensional" });
85.             }
86.         }
87. 
88.         void Scenario10_SearchMultipleResourceIds(ResourceContext context, string[] resourceIds)
89.         {
90.             this.Scenario10TextBlock.Text = "";
91.             var dimensionMap = ResourceManager.Current.MainResourceMap.GetSubtree("dimensions");
92. 
93.             foreach (var id in resourceIds)
94.             {
95.                 NamedResource namedResource;
96.                 if (dimensionMap.TryGetValue(id, out namedResource))
97.                 {
98.                     var resourceCandidates = namedResource.ResolveAll(context);
99.                     Scenario10_ShowCandidates(id, resourceCandidates);
100.                 }
101.             }
102.         }
103. 
104.         void Scenario10_ShowCandidates(string resourceId, IReadOnlyList<ResourceCandidate> resourceCandidates)
105.         {
106.             // print 'resourceId', 'found value', 'qualifer info', 'matching condition'
107.             string outText = "resourceId: dimensions\\" + resourceId + "\r\n";
108.             int i = 0;
109.             foreach (var candidate in resourceCandidates)
110.             {
111.                 var value = candidate.ValueAsString;
112.                 outText += "    Candidate " + i.ToString() + ":" + value + "\r\n";
113.                 int j = 0;
114.                 foreach (var qualifier in candidate.Qualifiers)
115.                 {
116.                     var qualifierName = qualifier.QualifierName;
117.                     var qualifierValue = qualifier.QualifierValue;
118.                     outText += "        Qualifer: " + qualifierName + ": " + qualifierValue + "\r\n";
119.                     outText += "        Matching: IsMatch (" + qualifier.IsMatch.ToString() + ")  " + "IsDefault (" + qualifier.IsDefault.ToString() + ")" + "\r\n";
120.                     j++;
121.                 }
122.                 i++;
123.             }
124. 
125.             this.Scenario10TextBlock.Text += outText + "\r\n";
126. 
127.         }
128. 
129.     }

11. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml;
12. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;
13. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Navigation;
14. using SDKTemplate;
15. using System;
16. using Windows.ApplicationModel.Resources.Core;

20. {
21.     /// <summary>
22.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
23.     /// </summary>
24.     public sealed partial class Scenario12 : Page
25.     {
26.         // A pointer back to the main page.  This is needed if you want to call methods in MainPage such
27.         // as NotifyUser()
28.         MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
29. 
30.         public Scenario12()
31.         {
32.             this.InitializeComponent();
33.         }
34. 
35.         /// <summary>
36.         /// Invoked when this page is about to be displayed in a Frame.
37.         /// </summary>
38.         /// <param name="e">Event data that describes how this page was reached.  The Parameter
39.         /// property is typically used to configure the page.</param>
40.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
41.         {
42.         }
43. 
44.         private async void Scenario12Button_Show_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
45.         {
46.             // Two coding patterns will be used:
47.             //   1. Get a ResourceContext on the UI thread using GetForCurrentView and pass 
48.             //      to the non-UI thread
49.             //   2. Get a ResourceContext on the non-UI thread using GetForViewIndependentUse
50.             //
51.             // Two analogous patterns could be used for ResourceLoader instead of ResourceContext.
52. 
53. 
54.             // pattern 1: get a ResourceContext for the UI thread
55.             // TODO[migrate-004]: see PATTERNS.md#getforcurrentview
56.             ResourceContext defaultContextForUiThread = ResourceContext.GetForCurrentView();
57. 
58.             // pattern 2: we'll create a view-independent context in the non-UI worker thread
59. 
60. 
61.             // We need some things in order to display results in the UI (doing that
62.             // for purposes of this sample, to show that work was actually done in the
63.             // worker thread):
64.             List<string> uiDependentResourceList = new List<string>();
65.             List<string> uiIndependentResourceList = new List<string>();
66. 
67. 
68.             // use a worker thread for the heavy lifting so the UI isn't blocked
69.             await Windows.System.Threading.ThreadPool.RunAsync(
70.                 (source) =>
71.                 {
72.                     ResourceMap stringResourceMap = ResourceManager.Current.MainResourceMap.GetSubtree("Resources");
73. 
74.                     // pattern 1: the defaultContextForUiThread variable was created above and is visible here
75. 
76.                     // pattern 2: get a view-independent ResourceContext
77.                     ResourceContext defaultViewIndependentResourceContext = ResourceContext.GetForViewIndependentUse();
78. 
79.                     // NOTE: The ResourceContext obtained using GetForViewIndependentUse() has no scale qualifier
80.                     // value set. If this ResourceContext is used in its default state to retrieve a resource, that 
81.                     // will work provided that the resource does not have any scale-qualified variants. But if the
82.                     // resource has any scale-qualified variants, then that will fail at runtime.
83.                     //
84.                     // A scale qualifier value on this ResourceContext can be set programmatically. If that is done,
85.                     // then the ResourceContext can be used to retrieve a resource that has scale-qualified variants.
86.                     // But if the scale qualifier is reset (e.g., using the ResourceContext.Reset() method), then
87.                     // it will return to the default state with no scale qualifier value set, and cannot be used
88.                     // to retrieve any resource that has scale-qualified variants.
89. 
90. 
91.                     // simulate processing a number of items
92.                     // just using a single string resource: that's sufficient to demonstrate 
93.                     for (var i = 0; i < 4; i++)
94.                     {
95.                         // pattern 1: use the ResourceContext from the UI thread
96.                         string listItem1 = stringResourceMap.GetValue("string1", defaultContextForUiThread).ValueAsString;
97.                         uiDependentResourceList.Add(listItem1);
98. 
99.                         // pattern 2: use the view-independent ResourceContext
100.                         string listItem2 = stringResourceMap.GetValue("string1", defaultViewIndependentResourceContext).ValueAsString;
101.                         uiIndependentResourceList.Add(listItem2);
102.                     }
103. 
104.                 });
105. 
106.             // Display the results in one go. (A more finessed design might add results
107.             // in the UI asynchronously, but that goes beyond what this sample is 
108.             // demonstrating.)
109.             ViewDependentResourcesList.ItemsSource = uiDependentResourceList;
110.             ViewIndependentResourcesList.ItemsSource = uiIndependentResourceList;
111.         }
112. 
113.     }
114. }
115.

55.         }
56.     }
57. }

--- RESULT ---
<a id="getforcurrentview"></a>
## GetForCurrentView() ReplacementsNone of the `GetForCurrentView()` patterns work in WinUI 3 desktop — there is no implicit per-view singleton.| UWP API | WinUI 3 Replacement |
|---------|---------------------|
| `ApplicationView.GetForCurrentView()` | `AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(windowId)` |
| `UIViewSettings.GetForCurrentView()` | `AppWindow` properties (size, presenter) |
| `DisplayInformation.GetForCurrentView()` | `XamlRoot.RasterizationScale` or Win32 `GetDpiForWindow` |
| `CoreApplication.GetCurrentView()` | Track windows manually in `App` |
| `SystemNavigationManager.GetForCurrentView()` | Wire back handling in `NavigationView` / `BackRequested` directly |
---DONE---
<exited with exit code 0>
--- RESULT ---
<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueueUWP (replace this):```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```WinUI 3:```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).
---DONE---

23.     /// Provides application-specific behavior to supplement the default Application class.
24.     /// </summary>
25.     sealed partial class App : Application
26.     {
27.         /// <summary>
28.         /// Initializes the singleton application object.  This is the first line of authored code
29.         /// executed, and as such is the logical equivalent of main() or WinMain().
30.         /// </summary>
31.         public App()
32.         {
33.             this.InitializeComponent();
34.             this.Construct();
35.         }
36. 
37.         /// <summary>
38.         /// Invoked when the application is launched normally by the end user.  Other entry points
39.         /// will be used such as when the application is launched to open a specific file.
40.         /// </summary>
41.         /// <param name="e">Details about the launch request and process.</param>
42.         protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs e)
43.         {
44. 
45. #if DEBUG
46.             if (System.Diagnostics.Debugger.IsAttached)
47.             {
48.                 this.DebugSettings.EnableFrameRateCounter = false;
49.             }
50. #endif
51. 
52.             Frame rootFrame = Window.Current.Content as Frame;
53. 
54.             // Do not repeat app initialization when the Window already has content,
55.             // just ensure that the window is active
56.             if (rootFrame == null)
57.             {
58.                 // Create a Frame to act as the navigation context and navigate to the first page
59.                 rootFrame = new Frame();
60.                 // Set the default language
61.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
62. 
63.                 rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
64. 
65.                 if (e.PreviousExecutionState == ApplicationExecutionState.Terminated)
66.                 {
67.                     //TODO: Load state from previously suspended application
68.                 }
69. 
70.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
71.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;
72.             }
73. 
74.             if (rootFrame.Content == null)
75.             {
76.                 // When the navigation stack isn't restored navigate to the first page,
77.                 // configuring the new page by passing required information as a navigation
78.                 // parameter
79.                 rootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage), e.Arguments);
80.             }
81. 
82.             // Ensure the current window is active
83.             Window.Current.Activate();
84. 
85.             // Some samples want access to the LaunchActivatedEventArgs.
86.             LaunchCompleted(e);
87.         }
88. 
89.         private Frame CreateRootFrame()
90.         {
91.             Frame rootFrame = Window.Current.Content as Frame;
92. 
93.             // Do not repeat app initialization when the Window already has content,
94.             // just ensure that the window is active
95.             if (rootFrame == null)
96.             {
97.                 // Create a Frame to act as the navigation context and navigate to the first page
98.                 rootFrame = new Frame();
99. 
100.                 // Set the default language
101.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
102.                 rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
103. 
104.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
105.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;
106.             }
107. 
108.             return rootFrame;
109.         }

41.                 This can be seen in
42.                 the File Resources in XAML scenario
43.                 when running on a multi-monitor system configured with a low-DPI monitor and a high-DPI 
44.                 monitor by dragging the app from one monitor to the other.

31.                 At times, having the ability to maintain separate resource files can be useful. While the default resource file is resources.resw, to load resources from additional files, 
32. string identifiers should use path-absolute URI references that start with '/' and the filename (e.g., /errors/scenario8error loads from the errors.resw file). Resource identifiers are URIs which are typically relative to ms-resource:///resources/, 
33. which represents the resources.resw file in the main package. Other packages can be referenced in the URI hostname (e.g. ms-resource://packagename/).
34.             </TextBlock>

34.                 Resources can depend on many different dimensions, including scale, language and
35.                 contrast. The resource management system does appropriate fallback behavior to retrieve
36.                 the most appropriate resource for the current context. The resource qualifiers are
37.                 evaluated in a priority based system (e.g., language is more important than scale).
38.                 Application developers are encouraged to mark resources as appropriately as possible.
39.                 Only resources that are appropriate for all contexts should be left without any
40.                 qualifiers. Default resources are qualified resources that are chosen if no resource
41.                 matches the context. The default language can be set by manually editing the .csproj 
42.                 file in a text editor.
43.             </TextBlock>
44.             <TextBlock x:Name="InputTextBlock2" Grid.Row="1" Margin="0,0,0,15"  TextWrapping="Wrap" Style="{StaticResource BasicTextStyle}" HorizontalAlignment="Left">

46.                 for different languages, scales, etc. Various contexts can be simulated with the
47.                 drop-down below to see which resources are matched in various situations. Each candidate
48.                 has additional metadata that can used to determine why it was chosen.
49.             </TextBlock>

34.                 When communicating with a web service that requires a single language to be specified,
35.                 one recommended technique for determining the language to send to the service is
36.                 to create a resource which represents the url or query parameter to send to the
37.                 service. This lets the resource management system make the appropriate choice for
38.                 the best language to send.
39.             </TextBlock>
40.             <TextBlock x:Name="InputTextBlock2" Grid.Row="1" TextWrapping="Wrap" Style="{StaticResource BasicTextStyle}" HorizontalAlignment="Left" Margin="0,0,0,15">
41.                 This achieves appropriate language matching, consistent fallback across the user's
42.                 preference list, and language consistency with the client side resources.
43.             </TextBlock>

38.                     Resource context values for some qualifiers, such as language or scale, are set automatically by the system. For
39.                     certain qualifiers, however, the app is expected to set context qualifier values as appropriate. The
40.                     DXFeatureLevel qualifier is like that. When an ms-appx URI is used to retrieve a file resource, the resource 
41.                     candidate is resolved using the default context of the thread in which the file is accessed. When using async APIs,
42.                     that file access may occur on a background thread. To ensure that the required DXFeatureLevel value is set on the 
43.                     default context used in the background thread, it should be set for the default context of all app threads using 
44.                     SetGlobalQualifierValue.

34.             <TextBlock x:Name="InputTextBlock1" TextWrapping="Wrap" Grid.Row="0" Style="{StaticResource BasicTextStyle}" HorizontalAlignment="Left" Margin="0,0,0,10" >
35.                 Apps may need to retrieve resources in non-UI threads. For example, if a large number of items are
36.                 being processed (for instance, adding watermark text to a number of images using a template resource
37.                 string), then a separate thread might be used to do this asynchronously so the UI isn't blocked.
38.             </TextBlock>
39.             <TextBlock x:Name="InputTextBlock2"  TextWrapping="Wrap" Grid.Row="1" Style="{StaticResource BasicTextStyle}" HorizontalAlignment="Left" Margin="0,0,0,10" >
40.                 In the general case, however, resources can be qualified for scale or other view-dependent 
41.                 qualifiers. But scale is ambiguous on a non-UI thread. For that reason, coding patterns to retrieve
42.                 resources that work on a UI thread, using the GetForCurrentView() method on the ResourceLoader or
43.                 ResourceContext class, will fail on a non-UI thread. Different coding patterns are needed.
44.             </TextBlock>
45.             <TextBlock x:Name="InputTextBlock3" TextWrapping="Wrap" Grid.Row="2" Style="{StaticResource BasicTextStyle}" HorizontalAlignment="Left" Margin="0,0,0,10">
46.                 Two coding patterns are available. If the resources to be retrieved may be qualified for scale or
47.                 other view-dependent qualifiers, then a ResourceLoader or ResourceContext object should be obtained 
48.                 on the UI thread where the resources will be used, then passed to the non-UI thread. If the resources 
49.                 are guaranteed not to use scale or other view-dependent qualifiers, then a view-independent 
50.                 ResourceLoader or ResourceContext object can be obtained on the non-UI thread using the 
51.                 GetForViewIndependentUse() method.
52.             </TextBlock>
53.             <TextBlock x:Name="InputTextBlock4" TextWrapping="Wrap" Grid.Row="3" Style="{StaticResource BasicTextStyle}" HorizontalAlignment="Left" Margin="0,0,0,10">
54.                 Click the button below to show resources retrieved on a non-UI thread using the two coding patterns
55.                 described above.
56.             </TextBlock>

1. //*********************************************************
2. //
3. // Copyright (c) Microsoft. All rights reserved.

11. using System;
12. using System.Collections.Generic;
13. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml;
14. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;
15. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Navigation;
16. using SDKTemplate;
17. using Windows.ApplicationModel.Resources.Core;
18. using System.Threading.Tasks;
19. using Windows.Storage;
20. 
21. namespace SDKTemplate
22. {
23.     /// <summary>
24.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
25.     /// </summary>
26.     public sealed partial class Scenario13 : Page
27.     {
28.         // A pointer back to the main page.  This is needed if you want to call methods in MainPage such
29.         // as NotifyUser()
30.         MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
31. 
32.         public Scenario13()
33.         {
34.             this.InitializeComponent();
35.             DXFLOptionCombo.SelectedIndex = 0;
36.         }
37. 
38.         /// <summary>
39.         /// Invoked when this page is about to be displayed in a Frame.
40.         /// </summary>
41.         /// <param name="e">Event data that describes how this page was reached.  The Parameter
42.         /// property is typically used to configure the page.</param>
43.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
44.         {
45.         }
46. 
47. 
48.         private async void DXFLOptionCombo_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
49.         {
50.             ComboBox combo = sender as ComboBox;
51. 
52.             // Setting the DXFeatureLevel qualifier value on all default contexts. This is needed
53.             // since the async operation to retrive appdata.dat as a StorageFile object will happen 
54.             // on a background thread. This is the only way to set the qualifier on a default context 
55.             // used in a background  thread.
56.             
57.             // In a different scenario, rather than obtaining a StorageFile object, another approach
58.             // that could be used would be to use the ResourceMap.GetValue method to get the resolved
59.             // resource candidate, and from that get the absolute file path. In that approach, the
60.             // GetValue method takes a ResourceContext parameter, and so it would be possible to set
61.             // the DXFeatureLevel qualifier value on a specific context rather than setting it in all
62.             // contexts within the app.
63. 
64.             ResourceContext.SetGlobalQualifierValue("dxfeaturelevel", combo.SelectedValue.ToString());
65. 
66.             // setting up the resource URI -- an ms-appx URI will be used to access the resource using
67.             // a StorageFile method
68. 
69.             // In a different scenario, rather than obtaining a StorageFile object, the 
70.             // ResourceMap.GetValue method could be used to get the resolved candidate, from which an 
71.             // absolute file path could be obtained. However, resource references used by the APIs in 
72.             // Windows.ApplicationModel.Resources and Windows.ApplicationModel.Resources.Core use 
73.             // ms-resource URIs, not ms-appx URIs. Within a resource map, files are always organized 
74.             // under a "Files" top-level subtree. The ms-resource URI string for the file being accessed 
75.             // here would be "ms-resource:///Files/appdata/appdata.dat".
76. 
77.             // The URI string "ms-appx:///..." is rooted at the app package root.
78.             Uri uri = new Uri("ms-appx:///appdata/appdata.dat");
79. 
80.             // The resource candidate will be resolved during the call to GetFileFromApplicationUriAsync 
81.             // using the default context from a background thread.
82. 
83.             // File IO uses asynchronous techniques. For more info, see
84.             // http://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/windows/apps/hh464924.aspx
85. 
86.             StorageFile file = await StorageFile.GetFileFromApplicationUriAsync(uri);
87.             string content = await FileIO.ReadTextAsync(file);
88.             ResultText.Text = content;
89.         }
90. 
91. 
92.     } // end of class
93. }

    <resheader name="reader">System.Resources.ResXResourceReader, System.Windows.Forms, ...</resheader>
    <resheader name="writer">System.Resources.ResXResourceWriter, System.Windows.Forms, ...</resheader>
    <data name="Name1"><value>this is my long string</value><comment>this is a comment</comment></data>
    <data name="Color1" type="System.Drawing.Color, System.Drawing">Blue</data>
    <data name="Bitmap1" mimetype="application/x-microsoft.net.object.binary.base64">
        <value>[base64 mime encoded serialized .NET Framework object]</value>
    </data>
    <data name="Icon1" type="System.Drawing.Icon, System.Drawing" mimetype="application/x-microsoft.net.object.bytearray.base64">
        <value>[base64 mime encoded string representing a byte array form of the .NET Framework object]</value>
        <comment>This is a comment</comment>
    </data>

    There are any number of "resheader" rows that contain simple 
    name/value pairs.
    
    Each data row contains a name, and value. The row also contains a 
    type or mimetype. Type corresponds to a .NET class that support 
    text/value conversion through the TypeConverter architecture. 
    Classes that don't support this are serialized and stored with the 
    mimetype set.
    
    The mimetype is used for serialized objects, and tells the 
    ResXResourceReader how to depersist the object. This is currently not 
    extensible. For a given mimetype the value must be set accordingly:
    
    Note - application/x-microsoft.net.object.binary.base64 is the format 
    that the ResXResourceWriter will generate, however the reader can 
    read any of the formats listed below.
    
    mimetype: application/x-microsoft.net.object.binary.base64
    value   : The object must be serialized with 
            : System.Runtime.Serialization.Formatters.Binary.BinaryFormatter
            : and then encoded with base64 encoding.
    
    mimetype: application/x-microsoft.net.object.soap.base64
    value   : The object must be serialized with 
            : System.Runtime.Serialization.Formatters.Soap.SoapFormatter
            : and then encoded with base64 encoding.    mimetype: application/x-microsoft.net.object.bytearray.base64
    value   : The object must be serialized into a byte array 
            : using a System.ComponentModel.TypeConverter
            : and then encoded with base64 encoding.
    -->

    Microsoft ResX Schema 
    
    Version 2.0
    
    The primary goals of this format is to allow a simple XML format 
    that is mostly human readable. The generation and parsing of the 
    various data types are done through the TypeConverter classes 
    associated with the data types.
    
    Example:

        <value>[base64 mime encoded serialized .NET Framework object]</value>
    </data>
    <data name="Icon1" type="System.Drawing.Icon, System.Drawing" mimetype="application/x-microsoft.net.object.bytearray.base64">
        <value>[base64 mime encoded string representing a byte array form of the .NET Framework object]</value>
        <comment>This is a comment</comment>

    There are any number of "resheader" rows that contain simple 
    name/value pairs.
    
    Each data row contains a name, and value. The row also contains a 
    type or mimetype. Type corresponds to a .NET class that support 
    text/value conversion through the TypeConverter architecture. 
    Classes that don't support this are serialized and stored with the 
    mimetype set.
    
    The mimetype is used for serialized objects, and tells the 
    ResXResourceReader how to depersist the object. This is currently not 
    extensible. For a given mimetype the value must be set accordingly:
    
    Note - application/x-microsoft.net.object.binary.base64 is the format 
    that the ResXResourceWriter will generate, however the reader can 
    read any of the formats listed below.

    value   : The object must be serialized with

            : and then encoded with base64 encoding.    mimetype: application/x-microsoft.net.object.bytearray.base64
    value   : The object must be serialized into a byte array

21.     /// <summary>
22.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
23.     /// </summary>
24.     public sealed partial class Scenario10 : Page
25.     {
26.         // A pointer back to the main page.  This is needed if you want to call methods in MainPage such
27.         // as NotifyUser()

31.         {
32.             this.InitializeComponent();
33.         }
34. 
35.         /// <summary>
36.         /// Invoked when this page is about to be displayed in a Frame.
37.         /// </summary>
38.         /// <param name="e">Event data that describes how this page was reached.  The Parameter
39.         /// property is typically used to configure the page.</param>
40.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
41.         {
42.         }

44.         /// <summary>
45.         /// This is the click handler for the 'Scenario10Button_Show' button.  You would replace this with your own handler
46.         /// if you have a button or buttons on this page.
47.         /// </summary>
48.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
49.         /// <param name="e"></param>
50.         private void Scenario10Button_Show_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
51.         {
52.             Button b = sender as Button;
53.             if (b != null)
54.             {
55.                 // use a cloned context for this scenario so that qualifier values set
56.                 // in this scenario don't impact behaviour in other scenarios that
57.                 // use a default context for the view (crossover effects between
58.                 // the scenarios will not be expected)
59.                 // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#getforcurrentview
60.                 var context = ResourceContext.GetForCurrentView().Clone();
61. 
62.                 var selectedLanguage = Scenario10ComboBox_Language.SelectedValue;
63.                 var selectedScale = Scenario10ComboBox_Scale.SelectedValue;
64.                 var selectedContrast = Scenario10ComboBox_Contrast.SelectedValue;
65.                 var selectedHomeRegion = Scenario10ComboBox_HomeRegion.SelectedValue;
66. 
67.                 if (selectedLanguage != null)
68.                 {
69.                     context.QualifierValues["language"] = selectedLanguage.ToString();
70.                 }
71.                 if (selectedScale != null)
72.                 {
73.                     context.QualifierValues["scale"] = selectedScale.ToString();
74.                 }
75.                 if (selectedContrast != null)
76.                 {
77.                     context.QualifierValues["contrast"] = selectedContrast.ToString();
78.                 }
79.                 if (selectedHomeRegion != null)
80.                 {
81.                     context.QualifierValues["homeregion"] = selectedHomeRegion.ToString();
82.                 }
83. 
84.                 Scenario10_SearchMultipleResourceIds(context, new string[] { "LanguageOnly", "ScaleOnly", "ContrastOnly", "HomeRegionOnly", "MultiDimensional" });
85.             }
86.         }
87. 
88.         void Scenario10_SearchMultipleResourceIds(ResourceContext context, string[] resourceIds)
89.         {
90.             this.Scenario10TextBlock.Text = "";
91.             var dimensionMap = ResourceManager.Current.MainResourceMap.GetSubtree("dimensions");
92. 
93.             foreach (var id in resourceIds)
94.             {
95.                 NamedResource namedResource;
96.                 if (dimensionMap.TryGetValue(id, out namedResource))
97.                 {
98.                     var resourceCandidates = namedResource.ResolveAll(context);
99.                     Scenario10_ShowCandidates(id, resourceCandidates);
100.                 }
101.             }
102.         }
103. 
104.         void Scenario10_ShowCandidates(string resourceId, IReadOnlyList<ResourceCandidate> resourceCandidates)
105.         {
106.             // print 'resourceId', 'found value', 'qualifer info', 'matching condition'
107.             string outText = "resourceId: dimensions\\" + resourceId + "\r\n";
108.             int i = 0;
109.             foreach (var candidate in resourceCandidates)
110.             {
111.                 var value = candidate.ValueAsString;
112.                 outText += "    Candidate " + i.ToString() + ":" + value + "\r\n";
113.                 int j = 0;
114.                 foreach (var qualifier in candidate.Qualifiers)
115.                 {
116.                     var qualifierName = qualifier.QualifierName;
117.                     var qualifierValue = qualifier.QualifierValue;
118.                     outText += "        Qualifer: " + qualifierName + ": " + qualifierValue + "\r\n";
119.                     outText += "        Matching: IsMatch (" + qualifier.IsMatch.ToString() + ")  " + "IsDefault (" + qualifier.IsDefault.ToString() + ")" + "\r\n";
120.                     j++;
121.                 }
122.                 i++;
123.             }
124. 
125.             this.Scenario10TextBlock.Text += outText + "\r\n";
126. 
127.         }
128. 
129.     }
130. }

11. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml;
12. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;
13. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Navigation;
14. using SDKTemplate;
15. using System;
16. using Windows.ApplicationModel.Resources.Core;
17. using System.Collections.Generic;
18. 
19. namespace SDKTemplate
20. {
21.     /// <summary>
22.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
23.     /// </summary>
24.     public sealed partial class Scenario12 : Page
25.     {
26.         // A pointer back to the main page.  This is needed if you want to call methods in MainPage such
27.         // as NotifyUser()
28.         MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
29. 
30.         public Scenario12()
31.         {
32.             this.InitializeComponent();
33.         }
34. 
35.         /// <summary>
36.         /// Invoked when this page is about to be displayed in a Frame.
37.         /// </summary>
38.         /// <param name="e">Event data that describes how this page was reached.  The Parameter
39.         /// property is typically used to configure the page.</param>
40.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
41.         {
42.         }
43. 
44.         private async void Scenario12Button_Show_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
45.         {
46.             // Two coding patterns will be used:
47.             //   1. Get a ResourceContext on the UI thread using GetForCurrentView and pass 
48.             //      to the non-UI thread
49.             //   2. Get a ResourceContext on the non-UI thread using GetForViewIndependentUse
50.             //
51.             // Two analogous patterns could be used for ResourceLoader instead of ResourceContext.
52. 
53. 
54.             // pattern 1: get a ResourceContext for the UI thread
55.             // TODO[migrate-004]: see PATTERNS.md#getforcurrentview
56.             ResourceContext defaultContextForUiThread = ResourceContext.GetForCurrentView();
57. 
58.             // pattern 2: we'll create a view-independent context in the non-UI worker thread
59. 
60. 
61.             // We need some things in order to display results in the UI (doing that
62.             // for purposes of this sample, to show that work was actually done in the
63.             // worker thread):
64.             List<string> uiDependentResourceList = new List<string>();
65.             List<string> uiIndependentResourceList = new List<string>();
66. 
67. 
68.             // use a worker thread for the heavy lifting so the UI isn't blocked
69.             await Windows.System.Threading.ThreadPool.RunAsync(
70.                 (source) =>
71.                 {
72.                     ResourceMap stringResourceMap = ResourceManager.Current.MainResourceMap.GetSubtree("Resources");
73. 
74.                     // pattern 1: the defaultContextForUiThread variable was created above and is visible here
75. 
76.                     // pattern 2: get a view-independent ResourceContext
77.                     ResourceContext defaultViewIndependentResourceContext = ResourceContext.GetForViewIndependentUse();
78. 
79.                     // NOTE: The ResourceContext obtained using GetForViewIndependentUse() has no scale qualifier
80.                     // value set. If this ResourceContext is used in its default state to retrieve a resource, that 
81.                     // will work provided that the resource does not have any scale-qualified variants. But if the
82.                     // resource has any scale-qualified variants, then that will fail at runtime.
83.                     //
84.                     // A scale qualifier value on this ResourceContext can be set programmatically. If that is done,
85.                     // then the ResourceContext can be used to retrieve a resource that has scale-qualified variants.
86.                     // But if the scale qualifier is reset (e.g., using the ResourceContext.Reset() method), then
87.                     // it will return to the default state with no scale qualifier value set, and cannot be used
88.                     // to retrieve any resource that has scale-qualified variants.
89. 
90. 
91.                     // simulate processing a number of items
92.                     // just using a single string resource: that's sufficient to demonstrate 
93.                     for (var i = 0; i < 4; i++)
94.                     {
95.                         // pattern 1: use the ResourceContext from the UI thread
96.                         string listItem1 = stringResourceMap.GetValue("string1", defaultContextForUiThread).ValueAsString;
97.                         uiDependentResourceList.Add(listItem1);
98. 
99.                         // pattern 2: use the view-independent ResourceContext
100.                         string listItem2 = stringResourceMap.GetValue("string1", defaultViewIndependentResourceContext).ValueAsString;
101.                         uiIndependentResourceList.Add(listItem2);
102.                     }
103. 
104.                 });
105. 
106.             // Display the results in one go. (A more finessed design might add results
107.             // in the UI asynchronously, but that goes beyond what this sample is 
108.             // demonstrating.)
109.             ViewDependentResourcesList.ItemsSource = uiDependentResourceList;
110.             ViewIndependentResourcesList.ItemsSource = uiIndependentResourceList;
111.         }
112. 
113.     }
114. }
115.

13. using System.Text;
14. using Windows.ApplicationModel.Resources.Core;
15. using Windows.Globalization;
16. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml;
17. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;
18. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Navigation;

20. namespace SDKTemplate
21. {
22.     /// <summary>
23.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
24.     /// </summary>
25.     public sealed partial class Scenario8 : Page
26.     {
27.         int lastSelectedIndex;
28. 
29.         public Scenario8()
30.         {
31.             this.InitializeComponent();
32.             PopulateComboBox();
33.             UpdateCurrentAppLanguageMessage();
34.         }
35. 
36.         private void ShowText()
37.         {
38.             // TODO[migrate-010]: see PATTERNS.md#getforcurrentview
39.             ResourceContext defaultContextForCurrentView = ResourceContext.GetForCurrentView();
40.             ResourceMap stringResourcesResourceMap = ResourceManager.Current.MainResourceMap.GetSubtree("Resources");
41.             Scenario8MessageTextBlock.Text = stringResourcesResourceMap.GetValue("string1", defaultContextForCurrentView).ValueAsString;
42.         }
43. 
44.         private void LanguageOverrideCombo_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
45.         {
46.             var combo = sender as ComboBox;
47.             var item = combo.SelectedValue as ComboBoxItem;
48.             var languageTag = item.Tag as string;
49. 
50.             // Ignore the divider (tag = "-")
51.             if (languageTag == "-")
52.             {
53.                 combo.SelectedIndex = lastSelectedIndex;
54.             }
55.             else
56.             {
57.                 lastSelectedIndex = combo.SelectedIndex;
58. 
59.                 // Set the persistent language override
60.                 ApplicationLanguages.PrimaryLanguageOverride = languageTag;
61. 
62.                 // update current app languages message
63.                 UpdateCurrentAppLanguageMessage();
64.             }
65.         }
66. 
67.         void AddItemForLanguageTag(string languageTag)
68.         {
69.             var language = new Language(languageTag);
70.             var item = new ComboBoxItem { Content = language.DisplayName, Tag = languageTag };
71.             LanguageOverrideCombo.Items.Add(item);
72. 
73.             // Select this item if it is the primary language override.
74.             if (languageTag == ApplicationLanguages.PrimaryLanguageOverride)
75.             {
76.                 LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectedItem = item;
77.             }
78.         }
79. 
80.         void PopulateComboBox()
81.         {
82.             // First show the default setting
83.             LanguageOverrideCombo.Items.Add(new ComboBoxItem { Content = "Use language preferences (recommended)", Tag = "" });
84.             LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectedIndex = 0;
85. 
86.             // If there are app languages that the user speaks, show them next
87. 
88.             // Note: the first (non-override) language, if set as the primary language override
89.             // would give the same result as not having any primary language override. There's
90.             // still a difference, though: If the user changes their language preferences, the 
91.             // default setting (no override) would mean that the actual primary app language
92.             // could change. But if it's set as an override, then it will remain the primary
93.             // app language after the user changes their language preferences.
94. 
95.             foreach (var languageTag in ApplicationLanguages.Languages)
96.             {
97.                 AddItemForLanguageTag(languageTag);
98.             }
99. 
100.             // Now add a divider followed by all the app manifest languages (in case the user
101.             // wants to pick a language not currently in their profile)
102. 
103.             // NOTE: If an app is deployed using a bundle with resource packages, the following
104.             // addition to the list may not be useful: The set of languages returned by 
105.             // ApplicationLanguages.ManifestLanguages will consist of only those manifest 
106.             // languages in the main app package or in the resource packages that are installed 
107.             // and registered for the current user. Language resource packages get deployed for 
108.             // the user if the language is in the user's profile. Therefore, the only difference 
109.             // from the set returned by ApplicationLanguages.Languages above would depend on 
110.             // which languages are included in the main app package.
111. 
112.             LanguageOverrideCombo.Items.Add(new ComboBoxItem { Content = "——————", Tag = "-" });
113. 
114.             // In a production app, this list should be sorted, but that's beyond the
115.             // focus of this sample.
116.             foreach (var languageTag in ApplicationLanguages.ManifestLanguages)
117.             {
118.                 AddItemForLanguageTag(languageTag);
119.             }
120. 
121.             LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectionChanged += LanguageOverrideCombo_SelectionChanged;
122. 
123.             lastSelectedIndex = LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectedIndex;
124.         }
125. 
126.         private void UpdateCurrentAppLanguageMessage()
127.         {
128.             this.Scenario8AppLanguagesTextBlock.Text = "Current app language(s): " + GetAppLanguagesAsFormattedString();
129.         }
130. 
131.         private string GetAppLanguagesAsFormattedString()
132.         {
133.             return String.Join(", ", ApplicationLanguages.Languages);
134.         }
135.     }
136. }

52.             Button b = sender as Button;
53.             if (b != null)
54.             {
55.                 // use a cloned context for this scenario so that qualifier values set
56.                 // in this scenario don't impact behaviour in other scenarios that
57.                 // use a default context for the view (crossover effects between
58.                 // the scenarios will not be expected)
59.                 // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#getforcurrentview
60.                 var context = ResourceContext.GetForCurrentView().Clone();
61. 
62.                 var selectedLanguage = Scenario10ComboBox_Language.SelectedValue;
63.                 var selectedScale = Scenario10ComboBox_Scale.SelectedValue;
64.                 var selectedContrast = Scenario10ComboBox_Contrast.SelectedValue;
65.                 var selectedHomeRegion = Scenario10ComboBox_HomeRegion.SelectedValue;
66. 
67.                 if (selectedLanguage != null)
68.                 {
69.                     context.QualifierValues["language"] = selectedLanguage.ToString();
70.                 }
71.                 if (selectedScale != null)
72.                 {
73.                     context.QualifierValues["scale"] = selectedScale.ToString();
74.                 }
75.                 if (selectedContrast != null)
76.                 {
77.                     context.QualifierValues["contrast"] = selectedContrast.ToString();
78.                 }
79.                 if (selectedHomeRegion != null)
80.                 {
81.                     context.QualifierValues["homeregion"] = selectedHomeRegion.ToString();
82.                 }
83. 
84.                 Scenario10_SearchMultipleResourceIds(context, new string[] { "LanguageOnly", "ScaleOnly", "ContrastOnly", "HomeRegionOnly", "MultiDimensional" });
85.             }
86.         }

88.         void Scenario10_SearchMultipleResourceIds(ResourceContext context, string[] resourceIds)
89.         {
90.             this.Scenario10TextBlock.Text = "";
91.             var dimensionMap = ResourceManager.Current.MainResourceMap.GetSubtree("dimensions");
92. 
93.             foreach (var id in resourceIds)
94.             {
95.                 NamedResource namedResource;
96.                 if (dimensionMap.TryGetValue(id, out namedResource))
97.                 {
98.                     var resourceCandidates = namedResource.ResolveAll(context);
99.                     Scenario10_ShowCandidates(id, resourceCandidates);
100.                 }
101.             }
102.         }
103. 
104.         void Scenario10_ShowCandidates(string resourceId, IReadOnlyList<ResourceCandidate> resourceCandidates)
105.         {
106.             // print 'resourceId', 'found value', 'qualifer info', 'matching condition'
107.             string outText = "resourceId: dimensions\\" + resourceId + "\r\n";
108.             int i = 0;
109.             foreach (var candidate in resourceCandidates)
110.             {
111.                 var value = candidate.ValueAsString;
112.                 outText += "    Candidate " + i.ToString() + ":" + value + "\r\n";
113.                 int j = 0;
114.                 foreach (var qualifier in candidate.Qualifiers)
115.                 {
116.                     var qualifierName = qualifier.QualifierName;
117.                     var qualifierValue = qualifier.QualifierValue;
118.                     outText += "        Qualifer: " + qualifierName + ": " + qualifierValue + "\r\n";
119.                     outText += "        Matching: IsMatch (" + qualifier.IsMatch.ToString() + ")  " + "IsDefault (" + qualifier.IsDefault.ToString() + ")" + "\r\n";
120.                     j++;
121.                 }
122.                 i++;
123.             }
124. 
125.             this.Scenario10TextBlock.Text += outText + "\r\n";
126. 
127.         }
128. 
129.     }

21.     /// <summary>
22.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
23.     /// </summary>
24.     public sealed partial class Scenario12 : Page
25.     {
26.         // A pointer back to the main page.  This is needed if you want to call methods in MainPage such
27.         // as NotifyUser()
28.         MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
29. 
30.         public Scenario12()
31.         {
32.             this.InitializeComponent();
33.         }
34. 
35.         /// <summary>
36.         /// Invoked when this page is about to be displayed in a Frame.
37.         /// </summary>
38.         /// <param name="e">Event data that describes how this page was reached.  The Parameter
39.         /// property is typically used to configure the page.</param>
40.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
41.         {
42.         }
43. 
44.         private async void Scenario12Button_Show_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
45.         {
46.             // Two coding patterns will be used:
47.             //   1. Get a ResourceContext on the UI thread using GetForCurrentView and pass 
48.             //      to the non-UI thread
49.             //   2. Get a ResourceContext on the non-UI thread using GetForViewIndependentUse
50.             //
51.             // Two analogous patterns could be used for ResourceLoader instead of ResourceContext.
52. 
53. 
54.             // pattern 1: get a ResourceContext for the UI thread
55.             // TODO[migrate-004]: see PATTERNS.md#getforcurrentview
56.             ResourceContext defaultContextForUiThread = ResourceContext.GetForCurrentView();
57. 
58.             // pattern 2: we'll create a view-independent context in the non-UI worker thread
59. 
60. 
61.             // We need some things in order to display results in the UI (doing that
62.             // for purposes of this sample, to show that work was actually done in the
63.             // worker thread):
64.             List<string> uiDependentResourceList = new List<string>();
65.             List<string> uiIndependentResourceList = new List<string>();
66. 
67. 
68.             // use a worker thread for the heavy lifting so the UI isn't blocked
69.             await Windows.System.Threading.ThreadPool.RunAsync(
70.                 (source) =>
71.                 {
72.                     ResourceMap stringResourceMap = ResourceManager.Current.MainResourceMap.GetSubtree("Resources");
73. 
74.                     // pattern 1: the defaultContextForUiThread variable was created above and is visible here
75. 
76.                     // pattern 2: get a view-independent ResourceContext
77.                     ResourceContext defaultViewIndependentResourceContext = ResourceContext.GetForViewIndependentUse();
78. 
79.                     // NOTE: The ResourceContext obtained using GetForViewIndependentUse() has no scale qualifier
80.                     // value set. If this ResourceContext is used in its default state to retrieve a resource, that 
81.                     // will work provided that the resource does not have any scale-qualified variants. But if the
82.                     // resource has any scale-qualified variants, then that will fail at runtime.
83.                     //
84.                     // A scale qualifier value on this ResourceContext can be set programmatically. If that is done,
85.                     // then the ResourceContext can be used to retrieve a resource that has scale-qualified variants.
86.                     // But if the scale qualifier is reset (e.g., using the ResourceContext.Reset() method), then
87.                     // it will return to the default state with no scale qualifier value set, and cannot be used
88.                     // to retrieve any resource that has scale-qualified variants.
89. 
90. 
91.                     // simulate processing a number of items
92.                     // just using a single string resource: that's sufficient to demonstrate 
93.                     for (var i = 0; i < 4; i++)
94.                     {
95.                         // pattern 1: use the ResourceContext from the UI thread
96.                         string listItem1 = stringResourceMap.GetValue("string1", defaultContextForUiThread).ValueAsString;
97.                         uiDependentResourceList.Add(listItem1);
98. 
99.                         // pattern 2: use the view-independent ResourceContext
100.                         string listItem2 = stringResourceMap.GetValue("string1", defaultViewIndependentResourceContext).ValueAsString;
101.                         uiIndependentResourceList.Add(listItem2);
102.                     }
103. 
104.                 });
105. 
106.             // Display the results in one go. (A more finessed design might add results
107.             // in the UI asynchronously, but that goes beyond what this sample is 
108.             // demonstrating.)
109.             ViewDependentResourcesList.ItemsSource = uiDependentResourceList;
110.             ViewIndependentResourcesList.ItemsSource = uiIndependentResourceList;
111.         }
112. 
113.     }
114. }
115.

21. {
22.     /// <summary>
23.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
24.     /// </summary>
25.     public sealed partial class Scenario8 : Page
26.     {
27.         int lastSelectedIndex;
28. 
29.         public Scenario8()
30.         {
31.             this.InitializeComponent();
32.             PopulateComboBox();
33.             UpdateCurrentAppLanguageMessage();
34.         }
35. 
36.         private void ShowText()
37.         {
38.             // TODO[migrate-010]: see PATTERNS.md#getforcurrentview
39.             ResourceContext defaultContextForCurrentView = ResourceContext.GetForCurrentView();
40.             ResourceMap stringResourcesResourceMap = ResourceManager.Current.MainResourceMap.GetSubtree("Resources");
41.             Scenario8MessageTextBlock.Text = stringResourcesResourceMap.GetValue("string1", defaultContextForCurrentView).ValueAsString;
42.         }
43. 
44.         private void LanguageOverrideCombo_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
45.         {
46.             var combo = sender as ComboBox;
47.             var item = combo.SelectedValue as ComboBoxItem;
48.             var languageTag = item.Tag as string;
49. 
50.             // Ignore the divider (tag = "-")
51.             if (languageTag == "-")
52.             {
53.                 combo.SelectedIndex = lastSelectedIndex;
54.             }
55.             else
56.             {
57.                 lastSelectedIndex = combo.SelectedIndex;
58. 
59.                 // Set the persistent language override
60.                 ApplicationLanguages.PrimaryLanguageOverride = languageTag;
61. 
62.                 // update current app languages message
63.                 UpdateCurrentAppLanguageMessage();
64.             }
65.         }
66. 
67.         void AddItemForLanguageTag(string languageTag)
68.         {
69.             var language = new Language(languageTag);
70.             var item = new ComboBoxItem { Content = language.DisplayName, Tag = languageTag };
71.             LanguageOverrideCombo.Items.Add(item);
72. 
73.             // Select this item if it is the primary language override.
74.             if (languageTag == ApplicationLanguages.PrimaryLanguageOverride)
75.             {
76.                 LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectedItem = item;
77.             }
78.         }
79. 
80.         void PopulateComboBox()
81.         {
82.             // First show the default setting
83.             LanguageOverrideCombo.Items.Add(new ComboBoxItem { Content = "Use language preferences (recommended)", Tag = "" });
84.             LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectedIndex = 0;
85. 
86.             // If there are app languages that the user speaks, show them next
87. 
88.             // Note: the first (non-override) language, if set as the primary language override
89.             // would give the same result as not having any primary language override. There's
90.             // still a difference, though: If the user changes their language preferences, the 
91.             // default setting (no override) would mean that the actual primary app language
92.             // could change. But if it's set as an override, then it will remain the primary
93.             // app language after the user changes their language preferences.
94. 
95.             foreach (var languageTag in ApplicationLanguages.Languages)
96.             {
97.                 AddItemForLanguageTag(languageTag);
98.             }
99. 
100.             // Now add a divider followed by all the app manifest languages (in case the user
101.             // wants to pick a language not currently in their profile)
102. 
103.             // NOTE: If an app is deployed using a bundle with resource packages, the following
104.             // addition to the list may not be useful: The set of languages returned by 
105.             // ApplicationLanguages.ManifestLanguages will consist of only those manifest 
106.             // languages in the main app package or in the resource packages that are installed 
107.             // and registered for the current user. Language resource packages get deployed for 
108.             // the user if the language is in the user's profile. Therefore, the only difference 
109.             // from the set returned by ApplicationLanguages.Languages above would depend on 
110.             // which languages are included in the main app package.
111. 
112.             LanguageOverrideCombo.Items.Add(new ComboBoxItem { Content = "——————", Tag = "-" });
113. 
114.             // In a production app, this list should be sorted, but that's beyond the
115.             // focus of this sample.
116.             foreach (var languageTag in ApplicationLanguages.ManifestLanguages)
117.             {
118.                 AddItemForLanguageTag(languageTag);
119.             }
120. 
121.             LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectionChanged += LanguageOverrideCombo_SelectionChanged;
122. 
123.             lastSelectedIndex = LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectedIndex;
124.         }
125. 
126.         private void UpdateCurrentAppLanguageMessage()
127.         {
128.             this.Scenario8AppLanguagesTextBlock.Text = "Current app language(s): " + GetAppLanguagesAsFormattedString();
129.         }
130. 
131.         private string GetAppLanguagesAsFormattedString()
132.         {
133.             return String.Join(", ", ApplicationLanguages.Languages);
134.         }
135.     }
136. }
